Appendix
\thechapter
.J
Computation details

In this appendix, we give RKHS and neural network derivations for Algorithm
2
:

\displaystyle(\hat{g},\hat{h})

\displaystyle=\argmin_{g\in\mathcal{G},h\in\mathcal{H}}\max_{f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[2\left\{g(A)-Y\right\}f^{\prime}(C^{\prime})-f^{\prime}(C^{\prime})^{2}\right]+\mu^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{n}\{g(A)^{2}\}

\displaystyle\quad+\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[2\left\{h(B)-g(A)\right\}f(C)-f(C)^{2}\right]+\mu\mathbb{E}_{n}\{h(B)^{2}\}.

Results for Algorithms
1
and
\thechapter.B.1
are similar and can be shared upon request. So can derivations for lasso and random forest function spaces.

In formal remarks, we also study the “subsetted” modification

\displaystyle(\hat{g},\hat{h})

\displaystyle=\argmin_{g\in\mathcal{G},h\in\mathcal{H}}\max_{f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{p}\left[2\left\{g(A)-Y\right\}f^{\prime}(C^{\prime})-f^{\prime}(C^{\prime})^{2}\right]+\mu^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{n}\{g(A)^{2}\}

\displaystyle\quad+\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[2\left\{h(B)-g(A)\right\}f(C)-f(C)^{2}\right]+\mu\mathbb{E}_{n}\{h(B)^{2}\}

where
[p]
and
[q]
partition
[n]=(1,...,n)
, so
p+q=n
. This modification applies the estimator to conditional moments over different subpopulations indexed by
[p]
and
[q]
.

For the index set
[p]
, let
I_{[p]}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times n}
be the matrix of ones and zeros such that
V_{[p]}=I_{[p]}V
gives the elements of
V
whose indices are in
[p]
.

Finally, we abbreviate
\mu I=\mu
and
\mu^{\prime}I=\mu^{\prime}
when it is clear from context.

\thechapter
.J.1
RKHS: Closed form

Let
V_{g,h}^{\prime}=g(A)-Y
and
V_{g,h}=h(B)-g(A)
. Let
\Phi_{C}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an operator with
i
th row
\langle\phi(C_{i}),\cdot\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}
. Define
\Phi_{C^{\prime}}
analogously, replacing
C_{i}
with
C_{i}^{\prime}
. Let
K_{C}
and
K_{C^{\prime}}
be the corresponding kernel matrices.

Maximizers.

Lemma
\thechapter
.J.1

(Existence of maximizers)
.

There exist coefficients
\hat{\gamma}_{g,h},\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{g,h}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that maximizers take the form
\hat{f}_{g,h}=\Phi_{C}^{*}\hat{\gamma}_{g,h}
and
\hat{f}^{\prime}_{g,h}=\Phi_{C^{\prime}}^{*}\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{g,h}
.

Remark
\thechapter
.J.1

(Subsetted estimator)
.

For the subsetted estimator, the same results hold but with
\hat{\gamma}_{g,h;[q]}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
and
\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{g,h;[p]}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, acting on appropriately modified feature operators
\Phi^{*}_{C;[q]}
and
\Phi^{*}_{C^{\prime};[p]}
.

Proof.

Write the objectives for the maximizers as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(f^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{n}\left\{2V^{\prime}_{g,h}f^{\prime}(C^{\prime})-f^{\prime}(C^{\prime})^{2}\right\},\quad\mathcal{E}(f)=\mathbb{E}_{n}\left\{2V_{g,h}f(C)-f(C)^{2}\right\}.

We prove the former result; the latter is similar. By the Riesz representation theorem,

\mathcal{E}(f)=\mathbb{E}_{n}\left\{2V_{g,h}\langle f,\phi(C)\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\langle f,\phi(C)\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}^{2}\right\}.

For an RKHS, evaluation is a continuous functional represented as the inner product with the feature map. Due to the ridge penalty, the stated objective has a maximizer
\hat{f}_{g,h}
that obtains the maximum.

To lighten notation, we suppress the indexing of
\hat{f}_{g,h}
by
(g,h)
for the rest of this argument. Write
\hat{f}=\hat{f}_{n}+\hat{f}^{\perp}_{n}
where
\hat{f}_{n}\in row(\Phi_{C})
and
\hat{f}_{n}^{\perp}\in null(\Phi_{C})
. Substituting this decomposition of
\hat{f}
into the objective, we see that

\mathcal{E}(\hat{f})=\mathcal{E}(\hat{f}_{n}).

Hence if
\hat{f}
is a maximizer, then there exists
\hat{f}_{n}
that is also a maximizer.
∎

Lemma
\thechapter
.J.2

(Formula of maximizers)
.

The explicit formula for the coefficients is
\hat{\gamma}_{g,h}=K_{C}^{\dagger}\vec{V}_{g,h}
and
\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{g,h}=K_{C^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h}
.

Remark
\thechapter
.J.2

(Subsetted estimator)
.

For the subsetted estimator, the same results hold but with
\hat{\gamma}_{g,h;[q]}=K_{C;[q,q]}^{\dagger}\vec{V}_{g,h;[q]}
and
\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{g,h;[p]}=K_{C^{\prime};[p,p]}^{\dagger}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h;[p]}
.

Proof.

We prove the former result; the latter is similar. Write the objective as

\mathcal{E}(f)=2\langle f,\hat{\mu}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\langle f,\hat{T}_{C}f\rangle_{\mathcal{F}},

where
\hat{\mu}_{g,h}=\mathbb{E}_{n}\{V_{g,h}\phi(C)\}=\frac{1}{n}\Phi_{C}^{*}\vec{V}_{g,h}
and
\hat{T}_{C}=\mathbb{E}_{n}\{\phi(C)\otimes\phi(C)^{*}\}=\frac{1}{n}\Phi_{C}^{*}\Phi_{C}
. Hence by Lemma
\thechapter.J.1
,

\mathcal{E}(\gamma)=2\langle\Phi_{C}^{*}\gamma_{g,h},\hat{\mu}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\langle\Phi_{C}^{*}\gamma_{g,h},\hat{T}_{C}\Phi_{C}^{*}\gamma_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}=\frac{2}{n}\gamma_{g,h}^{\top}\Phi_{C}\Phi_{C}^{*}\vec{V}_{g,h}-\frac{1}{n}\gamma_{g,h}^{\top}\Phi_{C}\Phi_{C}^{*}\Phi_{C}\Phi_{C}^{*}\gamma_{g,h}.

Since
K_{C}=\Phi_{C}\Phi_{C}^{*}
, the first order condition yields
K_{C}\vec{V}_{g,h}=K_{C}^{2}\hat{\gamma}_{g,h}
, i.e.
\hat{\gamma}_{g,h}=K_{C}^{\dagger}\vec{V}_{g,h}
where
K_{C}^{\dagger}
is the pseudoinverse of
K_{C}
.
∎

\thechapter
.J.1.1
Minimizers

Let
\Phi_{A}:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an operator with
i
th row
\langle\phi(A_{i}),\cdot\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}
. Define
\Phi_{B}
analogously, replacing
A_{i}
with
B_{i}
. Let
K_{A}
and
K_{B}
be the corresponding kernel matrices.

Lemma
\thechapter
.J.3

(Existence of minimizers)
.

There exist coefficients
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that minimizers take the form
\hat{g}=\Phi_{A}^{*}\hat{\alpha}
and
\hat{h}=\Phi_{B}^{*}\hat{\beta}
.

Remark
\thechapter
.J.3

(Subsetted estimator)
.

The result remains true for the subsetted estimator.

Proof.

To begin, write the objective
\mathcal{E}(g,h)
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{n}\left\{2V^{\prime}_{g,h}\hat{f}_{g,f}^{\prime}(C^{\prime})-\hat{f}_{g,h}^{\prime}(C^{\prime})^{2}\right\}+\mu^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{n}\{g(A)^{2}\}+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left\{2V_{g,h}\hat{f}_{g,h}(C)-\hat{f}_{g,h}(C)^{2}\right\}+\mu\mathbb{E}_{n}\{h(B)^{2}\}.

By Lemmas
\thechapter.J.1
and
\thechapter.J.2
,

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{g,f}^{\prime}(C^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\langle\hat{f}_{g,f}^{\prime},\phi(C^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}=\langle\Phi_{C^{\prime}}^{*}K_{C^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h},\phi(C^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{g,h}(C)

\displaystyle=\langle\hat{f}_{g,f},\phi(C)\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}=\langle\Phi_{C}^{*}K_{C}^{\dagger}\vec{V}_{g,h},\phi(C)\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}.

Hence
(g,h)
only appear via
V^{\prime}_{g,h}=g(A)-Y
,
V_{g,h}=h(B)-g(A)
, and directly as
g(A)
and
h(B)
. In all of these expressions, they can be further expressed as
g(A)=\langle g,\phi(A)\rangle_{\mathcal{G}}
and
h(B)=\langle h,\phi(B)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}
, which is a linear functional. The overall objective is quadratic in such terms, so the stated objective has maximizers
(\hat{g},\hat{h})
that obtain the maximum.

By a similar argument to Lemma
\thechapter.J.1
, for any
(\hat{g},\hat{h})
attaining the maximum,
\mathcal{E}(\hat{g},\hat{h})=\mathcal{E}(\hat{g}_{n},\hat{h}_{n})
where
\hat{g}_{n}\in row(\Phi_{A})
and
\hat{h}_{n}\in row(\Phi_{B})
.
∎

Lemma
\thechapter
.J.4

(Properties of pseudo-inverse)
.

For any square symmetric matrix
K\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, its eigendecomposition is
K=U\Sigma U^{\top}
where
\Sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
has nonzero diagonal entries and
r\leq n
. Its pseudo-inverse is
K^{\dagger}=U\Sigma^{-1}U^{\top}
. Moreover
K^{\dagger}K=KK^{\dagger}=UU^{\top}
, which is a projection.

To lighten notation, let
K_{C}^{\dagger}K_{C}=P_{C}
.

Proposition
\thechapter
.J.1

(Formula of minimizers)
.

The explicit formula for the coefficients is

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}

\displaystyle=\left[K_{A}\left\{-P_{C}+\left(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime}\right)K_{A}\left(K_{B}P_{C}K_{A}\right)^{\dagger}K_{B}\left(P_{C}+\mu\right)\right\}K_{B}\right]^{\dagger}K_{A}P_{C^{\prime}}Y

\displaystyle\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\left(K_{B}P_{C}K_{A}\right)^{\dagger}K_{B}\left(P_{C}+\mu\right)K_{B}\hat{\beta}

Proof.

We proceed in steps.

1.

Write the objective
\mathcal{E}(g,h)
as

2\langle\hat{f}^{\prime}_{g,h},\hat{\mu}^{\prime}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\langle\hat{f}^{\prime}_{g,h},\hat{T}_{C^{\prime}}\hat{f}^{\prime}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\mu^{\prime}\langle g,\hat{T}_{A}g\rangle_{\mathcal{G}}+2\langle\hat{f}_{g,h},\hat{\mu}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\langle\hat{f}_{g,h},\hat{T}_{C}\hat{f}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\mu\langle h,\hat{T}_{B}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

where

\hat{\mu}^{\prime}_{g,h}=\frac{1}{n}\Phi_{C^{\prime}}^{*}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h}
,

\hat{\mu}_{g,h}=\frac{1}{n}\Phi_{C}^{*}\vec{V}_{g,h}
, and the covariance operators are defined analogously to Lemma
\thechapter.J.2
. Hence by Lemma
\thechapter.J.2
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(g,h)

\displaystyle=2\langle\Phi_{C^{\prime}}^{*}K_{C^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h},\hat{\mu}^{\prime}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\langle\Phi_{C^{\prime}}^{*}K_{C^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h},\hat{T}_{C^{\prime}}\Phi_{C^{\prime}}^{*}K_{C^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\mu^{\prime}\langle g,\hat{T}_{A}g\rangle_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle+2\langle\Phi_{C}^{*}K_{C}^{\dagger}\vec{V}_{g,h},\hat{\mu}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\langle\Phi_{C}^{*}K_{C}^{\dagger}\vec{V}_{g,h},\hat{T}_{C}\Phi_{C}^{*}K_{C}^{\dagger}\vec{V}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\mu\langle h,\hat{T}_{B}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}(\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h})^{\top}K_{C^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{C^{\prime}}\Phi_{C^{\prime}}^{*}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h}-\frac{1}{n}(\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h})^{\top}K_{C^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{C^{\prime}}\Phi_{C^{\prime}}^{*}\Phi_{C^{\prime}}\Phi_{C^{\prime}}^{*}K_{C^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h}+\mu^{\prime}\langle g,\hat{T}_{A}g\rangle_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\vec{V}_{g,h}^{\top}K_{C}^{\dagger}\Phi_{C}\Phi_{C}^{*}\vec{V}_{g,h}-\frac{1}{n}\vec{V}_{g,h}^{\top}K_{C}^{\dagger}\Phi_{C}\Phi_{C}^{*}\Phi_{C}\Phi_{C}^{*}K_{C}^{\dagger}\vec{V}_{g,h}+\mu\langle h,\hat{T}_{B}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}(\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h})^{\top}P_{C^{\prime}}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h}+\mu^{\prime}\langle g,\hat{T}_{A}g\rangle_{\mathcal{G}}+\frac{1}{n}\vec{V}_{g,h}^{\top}P_{C}\vec{V}_{g,h}+\mu\langle h,\hat{T}_{B}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

2.

Let
Y,G,H\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be defined with
G_{i}=g(A_{i})
and
H_{i}=h(B_{i})
. In this notation,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}(\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h})^{\top}P_{C^{\prime}}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}(Y^{\top}P_{C^{\prime}}Y-2G^{\top}P_{C^{\prime}}Y+G^{\top}P_{C^{\prime}}G),\quad\mu^{\prime}\langle g,\hat{T}_{A}g\rangle_{\mathcal{G}}=\frac{\mu^{\prime}}{n}G^{\top}G

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\vec{V}_{g,h}^{\top}P_{C}\vec{V}_{g,h}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}(H^{\top}P_{C}H-2G^{\top}P_{C}H+G^{\top}P_{C}G),\quad\mu\langle h,\hat{T}_{B}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\frac{\mu}{n}H^{\top}H.

Combining with
G=\Phi_{A}g=K_{A}\alpha
and
H=\Phi_{B}h=K_{B}\beta
from Lemma
\thechapter.J.3
,

\displaystyle n\mathcal{E}(\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=Y^{\top}P_{C^{\prime}}Y-2G^{\top}(P_{C^{\prime}}Y+P_{C}H)+G^{\top}(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime})G+H^{\top}(P_{C}+\mu)H

\displaystyle=Y^{\top}P_{C^{\prime}}Y-2\alpha^{\top}K_{A}(P_{C^{\prime}}Y+P_{C}K_{B}\beta)+\alpha^{\top}K_{A}(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime})K_{A}\alpha

\displaystyle\quad+\beta^{\top}K_{B}(P_{C}+\mu)K_{B}\beta.

3.

The first order conditions yield

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-2K_{A}(P_{C^{\prime}}Y+P_{C}K_{B}\hat{\beta})+2K_{A}(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime})K_{A}\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-2K_{B}P_{C}K_{A}\hat{\alpha}+2K_{B}(P_{C}+\mu)K_{B}\hat{\beta}.

Rearranging and taking pseudo-inverses, we arrive at two equations:

\displaystyle K_{A}P_{C^{\prime}}Y+K_{A}P_{C}K_{B}\hat{\beta}

\displaystyle=K_{A}(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime})K_{A}\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle K_{B}P_{C}K_{A}\hat{\alpha}=K_{B}(P_{C}+\mu)K_{B}\hat{\beta}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hat{\alpha}=\left(K_{B}P_{C}K_{A}\right)^{\dagger}K_{B}\left(P_{C}+\mu\right)K_{B}\hat{\beta}.

4.

Substituting the latter into the former,

K_{A}P_{C^{\prime}}Y+K_{A}P_{C}K_{B}\hat{\beta}=K_{A}(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime})K_{A}\left(K_{B}P_{C}K_{A}\right)^{\dagger}K_{B}\left(P_{C}+\mu\right)K_{B}\hat{\beta},

and solving for
\hat{\beta}
,

\hat{\beta}=\left[K_{A}\left\{-P_{C}+\left(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime}\right)K_{A}\left(K_{B}P_{C}K_{A}\right)^{\dagger}K_{B}\left(P_{C}+\mu\right)\right\}K_{B}\right]^{\dagger}K_{A}P_{C^{\prime}}Y.\qed

Remark
\thechapter
.J.4

(Subsetted estimator)
.

The explicit formula for the coefficients is

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}

\displaystyle=\left[K_{A}\left\{-\tilde{P}_{C}+\left(\tilde{P}_{C^{\prime}}+\tilde{P}_{C}+\mu^{\prime}\right)K_{A}\left(K_{B}\tilde{P}_{C}K_{A}\right)^{\dagger}K_{B}\left(\tilde{P}_{C}+\mu\right)\right\}K_{B}\right]^{\dagger}K_{A}\tilde{P}_{C^{\prime}}Y

\displaystyle\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\left(K_{B}\tilde{P}_{C}K_{A}\right)^{\dagger}K_{B}\left(\tilde{P}_{C}+\mu\right)K_{B}\hat{\beta}

where
\tilde{P}_{C^{\prime}}=\frac{n}{p}I_{[p]}^{\top}P_{C^{\prime};[p,p]}I_{[p]}
and
\tilde{P}_{C}=\frac{n}{q}I_{[q]}^{\top}P_{C;[q,q]}I_{[q]}
. Note that
P_{C^{\prime};[p,p]}=(K_{C^{\prime};[p,p]})^{\dagger}K_{C^{\prime};[p,p]}
and
K_{C^{\prime};[p,p]}=I_{[p]}K_{C^{\prime}}I_{[p]}^{\top}
.

Proof.

We proceed in steps.

1.

Write the objective
\mathcal{E}(g,h)
as

\displaystyle 2\langle\hat{f}^{\prime}_{g,h},\hat{\mu}^{\prime}_{g,h;[p]}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\langle\hat{f}^{\prime}_{g,h},\hat{T}_{C^{\prime};[p,p]}\hat{f}^{\prime}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\mu^{\prime}\langle g,\hat{T}_{A}g\rangle_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\quad+2\langle\hat{f}_{g,h},\hat{\mu}_{g,h;[q]}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\langle\hat{f}_{g,h},\hat{T}_{C;[q,q]}\hat{f}_{g,h}\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\mu\langle h,\hat{T}_{B}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

where

\hat{\mu}^{\prime}_{g,h;[p]}=\frac{1}{p}\Phi_{C^{\prime};[p]}^{*}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h;[p]}
,

\hat{\mu}_{g,h;[q]}=\frac{1}{q}\Phi_{C;[q]}^{*}\vec{V}_{g,h;[q]}
, and the covariance operators are defined analogously to Remark
\thechapter.J.2
. Hence by Remark
\thechapter.J.2
and the same argument as in Proposition
\thechapter.J.1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(g,h)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}(\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h;[p]})^{\top}P_{C^{\prime};[p,p]}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h;[p]}+\mu^{\prime}\langle g,\hat{T}_{A}g\rangle_{\mathcal{G}}+\frac{1}{q}\vec{V}_{g,h;[q]}^{\top}P_{C;[q,q]}\vec{V}_{g,h;[q]}+\mu\langle h,\hat{T}_{B}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

2.

Let
Y,G,H\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be defined with
G_{i}=g(A_{i})
and
H_{i}=h(B_{i})
as before. Now, let
\tilde{P}_{C^{\prime}}=\frac{n}{p}I_{[p]}^{\top}P_{C^{\prime};[p,p]}I_{[p]}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
and

\tilde{P}_{C}=\frac{n}{q}I_{[q]}^{\top}P_{C^{\prime};[q,q]}I_{[q]}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}(\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h;[p]})^{\top}P_{C^{\prime};[p,p]}\vec{V}^{\prime}_{g,h;[p]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}(Y^{\top}\tilde{P}_{C^{\prime}}Y-2G^{\top}\tilde{P}_{C^{\prime}}Y+G^{\top}\tilde{P}_{C^{\prime}}G)

\displaystyle\mu^{\prime}\langle g,\hat{T}_{A}g\rangle_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{\prime}}{n}G^{\top}G

\displaystyle\frac{1}{q}\vec{V}_{g,h;[q]}^{\top}P_{C;[q,q]}\vec{V}_{g,h;[q]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}(H^{\top}\tilde{P}_{C}H-2G^{\top}\tilde{P}_{C}H+G^{\top}\tilde{P}_{C}G)

\displaystyle\mu\langle h,\hat{T}_{B}h\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu}{n}H^{\top}H.

Hereafter we use the same argument as in Proposition
\thechapter.J.1
. ∎

\thechapter
.J.1.2
Nyström approximation

Computation of kernel methods may be demanding due to the inversions of matrices that scale with
n
such as
K_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
. One solution is Nyström approximation. We now provide alternative expressions for the minimizers
(\hat{g},\hat{h})
that lend themselves to Nyström approximation, then describe the procedure.

Lemma
\thechapter
.J.5

(Minimizer sufficient statistics)
.

The minimizers may be expressed as

\displaystyle\hat{g}

\displaystyle=\left(\Phi_{B}^{*}P_{C}\Phi_{A}\right)^{\dagger}\Phi_{B}^{*}(P_{C}+\mu)\Phi_{B}\hat{h}

\displaystyle\hat{h}

\displaystyle=\left[\Phi_{A}^{*}\left\{-P_{C}+\left(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime}\right)\Phi_{A}\left(\Phi_{B}^{*}P_{C}\Phi_{A}\right)^{\dagger}\Phi_{B}^{*}\left(P_{C}+\mu\right)\right\}\Phi_{B}\right]^{\dagger}\Phi_{A}^{*}P_{C^{\prime}}Y.

Proof.

We proceed in steps.

1.

By the proof of Proposition
\thechapter.J.1
,
with
G=\Phi_{A}g
and
H=\Phi_{B}h
,

\displaystyle n\mathcal{E}(g,h)

\displaystyle=Y^{\top}P_{C^{\prime}}Y-2G^{\top}(P_{C^{\prime}}Y+P_{C}H)+G^{\top}(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime})G+H^{\top}(P_{C}+\mu)H

\displaystyle=Y^{\top}P_{C^{\prime}}Y-2g^{*}\Phi_{A}^{*}(P_{C^{\prime}}Y+P_{C}\Phi_{B}h)+g^{*}\Phi_{A}^{*}(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime})\Phi_{A}g+h^{*}\Phi_{B}^{*}(P_{C}+\mu)\Phi_{B}h.

2.

Informally, the first order conditions yield

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-2\Phi_{A}^{*}(P_{C^{\prime}}Y+P_{C}\Phi_{B}\hat{h})+2\Phi_{A}^{*}(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime})\Phi_{A}\hat{g}

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-2\Phi_{B}^{*}P_{C}\Phi_{A}\hat{g}+2\Phi_{B}^{*}(P_{C}+\mu)\Phi_{B}\hat{h}.

See
De Vito and Caponnetto, 2005
for the formal way of deriving the first order condition, which incurs additional notation.
Rearranging and taking pseudo-inverses, we arrive at two equations:

\displaystyle\Phi_{A}^{*}(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime})\Phi_{A}\hat{g}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{A}^{*}(P_{C^{\prime}}Y+P_{C}\Phi_{B}\hat{h})

\displaystyle\Phi_{B}^{*}P_{C}\Phi_{A}\hat{g}=\Phi_{B}^{*}(P_{C}+\mu)\Phi_{B}\hat{h}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hat{g}=\left(\Phi_{B}^{*}P_{C}\Phi_{A}\right)^{\dagger}\Phi_{B}^{*}(P_{C}+\mu)\Phi_{B}\hat{h}.

3.

Substituting the latter into the former,

\Phi_{A}^{*}P_{C^{\prime}}Y+\Phi_{A}^{*}P_{C}\Phi_{B}\hat{h}=\Phi_{A}^{*}(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime})\Phi_{A}\left(\Phi_{B}^{*}P_{C}\Phi_{A}\right)^{\dagger}\Phi_{B}^{*}(P_{C}+\mu)\Phi_{B}\hat{h},

and solving for
\hat{h}
,

\hat{h}=\left[\Phi_{A}^{*}\left\{-P_{C}+\left(P_{C^{\prime}}+P_{C}+\mu^{\prime}\right)\Phi_{A}\left(\Phi_{B}^{*}P_{C}\Phi_{A}\right)^{\dagger}\Phi_{B}^{*}\left(P_{C}+\mu\right)\right\}\Phi_{B}\right]^{\dagger}\Phi_{A}^{*}P_{C^{\prime}}Y.\qed

Remark
\thechapter
.J.5

(Subsetted estimator)
.

The subsetted minimizers may be expressed as

\displaystyle\hat{g}

\displaystyle=\left(\Phi_{B}^{*}\tilde{P}_{C}\Phi_{A}\right)^{\dagger}\Phi_{B}^{*}(\tilde{P}_{C}+\mu)\Phi_{B}\hat{h}

\displaystyle\hat{h}

\displaystyle=\left[\Phi_{A}^{*}\left\{-\tilde{P}_{C}+\left(\tilde{P}_{C^{\prime}}+\tilde{P}_{C}+\mu^{\prime}\right)\Phi_{A}\left(\Phi_{B}^{*}\tilde{P}_{C}\Phi_{A}\right)^{\dagger}\Phi_{B}^{*}\left(\tilde{P}_{C}+\mu\right)\right\}\Phi_{B}\right]^{\dagger}\Phi_{A}^{*}\tilde{P}_{C^{\prime}}Y.

Proof.

The argument is analogous to Remark
\thechapter.J.4
.
∎

Lemma
\thechapter
.J.6

(Properties of pseudo-inverse)
.

Continuing the notation of Lemma
\thechapter.J.4
, if
\Phi=U\Sigma^{1/2}V^{\top}
and
K=\Phi\Phi^{*}
, then
P=UU^{\top}=K^{\dagger}K=\Phi\Phi^{\dagger}
. Remark
\thechapter.J.4
relates
\tilde{P}
to
P
.

Combining Lemmas
\thechapter.J.5
and
\thechapter.J.6
, we conclude that sufficient statistics for
(\hat{g},\hat{h})
are feature operators. Within the feature operator
\Phi
, the
i
th row
\langle\phi(X_{i}),\cdot\rangle
may be viewed as an infinite dimensional vector.

Nyström approximation is a way to approximate infinite dimensional vectors with finite dimensional ones. It uses the substitution

\phi(x)\mapsto\check{\phi}(x)=(K_{\mathcal{S}\mathcal{S}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}K_{\mathcal{S}x}
, where

\mathcal{S}
is a subset of
s=|\mathcal{S}|\ll n
observations called landmarks.
K_{\mathcal{S}\mathcal{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times s}
is defined such that
(K_{\mathcal{S}\mathcal{S}})_{ij}=k(X_{i},X_{j})
for
i,j\in\mathcal{S}
. Similarly,
K_{\mathcal{S}x}\in\mathbb{R}^{s}
is defined such that
(K_{\mathcal{S}x})_{i}=k(X_{i},x)
for
i\in\mathcal{S}
.

In summary, the approximate sufficient statistics are of the form
\check{\Phi}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s}
, i.e. a matrix whose
i
th row
\langle\check{\phi}(X_{i}),\cdot\rangle
may be viewed as a vector in
\mathbb{R}^{s}
.

\thechapter
.J.2
Neural network: Stochastic gradient descent

Suppose the function classes are neural networks. Algorithm
2
takes the form

\displaystyle(\hat{g},\hat{h})

\displaystyle=\arg\min_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\max_{\omega_{1},\omega_{2}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[2\left\{g_{\theta_{1}}(A)-Y\right\}f_{\omega_{1}}^{\prime}(C^{\prime})-f_{\omega_{1}}^{\prime}(C^{\prime})^{2}\right]+\mu^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{n}\{g_{\theta_{1}}(A)^{2}\}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[2\left\{h_{\theta_{2}}(B)-g_{\theta_{1}}(A)\right\}f_{\omega_{2}}(C)-f_{\omega_{2}}(C)^{2}\right]+\mu\mathbb{E}_{n}\{h_{\theta_{2}}(B)^{2}\}\bigg\}

where
\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\omega_{1},\omega_{2}
are weights of the neural networks.

We use the optimistic Adam algorithm of
Daskalakis et al., 2017
, which is a type of stochastic gradient descent. This approach is standard in the literature
(
Dikkala et al., 2020
)
.

Remark
\thechapter
.J.6

(Subsetted estimator)
.

To adapt the algorithm, it suffices to make one simple modification: for observations outside of the subset, we set the adversary’s loss to zero.
